Source list confirms these six colours require compulsory warnings 
www.businesscompanion.info
.
Required Warning Statement
Foods containing these colours must carry the warning:
“May have an adverse effect on activity and attention in children.” 
www.fedup.com.au
Evidence Context
EFSA evaluated the Southampton study and found limited evidence of a small effect on activity and attention in some children 
www.efsa.europa.eu
.
Product Flag
If product contains any of these colours:
“Contains an artificial colour that requires a warning label in the EU/UK.”

9. Product-Level Flagging System
When FoodLens AI scans a product, the system must check every ingredient against this registry.
Flag Type 1: Banned Elsewhere
Display:
“This product contains {ingredient}, which is not permitted for food use in {jurisdiction}.”
Example:
“This product contains titanium dioxide, which is not permitted as a food additive in the EU.” 
ec.europa.eu
Flag Type 2: Authorisation Revoked
Display:
“This product contains {ingredient}, whose food authorisation has been revoked in {jurisdiction}.”
Example:
“This product contains FD&C Red No. 3, whose U.S. food authorisation has been revoked by the FDA.” 
www.fda.gov
Flag Type 3: Restricted
Display:
“This ingredient is permitted in {jurisdiction} only under specified limits or conditions.”
Flag Type 4: Warning Label Required
Display:
“This product contains an ingredient that requires a warning label in {jurisdiction}.”
Example:
“This product contains Sunset Yellow FCF, which requires a warning label in the EU/UK.” 
www.businesscompanion.info
Flag Type 5: Compliance Period
Display:
“This ingredient is subject to a compliance deadline in {jurisdiction}.”
Example:
“Red No. 3 food products must be reformulated in the U.S. by January 15, 2027.” 
www.fda.gov
10. Verification Workflow
Every record must pass through this workflow.
Step 1: Source Discovery
Find official regulation, database entry, final rule, or authority guidance.
Step 2: Extract Core Facts
Extract:
Substance
Jurisdiction
Status
Date
Scope
Reason
Source URL
Step 3: Cross-Check
Compare at least two sources where possible.
Priority:
Official law
Official database
Official press release
Official scientific opinion
Trusted legal summary
Step 4: Classify Confidence
Confidence
	
Meaning


High
	
Direct official regulation or database


Medium
	
Official summary or trusted legal summary


Low
	
News article or secondary source only
Step 5: Human Review
Human review required if:
Status is “banned”
Source is secondary only
Jurisdiction is unclear
Compliance date is unclear
Substance has multiple legal forms
Product category exceptions exist

FoodLens AI — Build Continuation
Batch 2: Global Artificial Colour Regulatory Registry
This continues the global food safety data build.
Batch 1 created the high-priority banned / revoked / restricted / labelled registry.
Batch 2 now builds the Artificial Colour Registry.
This registry will power FoodLens AI product flags such as:
“Contains an artificial colour.”
“Contains a colour that requires a warning label in the EU/UK.”
“This colour is permitted in India under FSSAI rules for specified food categories.”
“This colour is not listed as an FDA-certified food colour in the United States.”
“This colour has limited evidence related to behaviour in some children.”
1. Batch 2 Objective
Build a structured global registry for artificial food colours.
For each colour, collect:
Common name
Scientific name
E number
INS number
Alternative names
Function
EU status
UK status
U.S. FDA status
India FSSAI status
Codex Alimentarius status
Warning label requirement
Acceptable Daily Intake, if established
Scientific evidence flags
Official source references
Verification confidence
2. Mandatory Truth Rules for Colour Data
Rule 1: No “Banned” Without Official Source
Do not label a colour as banned unless an official regulator says so.
Use:
“Not authorised”
“Not listed as permitted”
“Authorisation revoked”
“Restricted to specified food categories”
“Warning label required”
Rule 2: Jurisdiction Is Mandatory
Every status must be tied to a jurisdiction.
Example:
“Sunset Yellow FCF requires a warning label in the EU/UK.”
Not:
“Sunset Yellow is dangerous.”
Rule 3: Food Category Is Mandatory
Many colours are permitted only in certain food categories.
Example:
“Permitted in specified food categories under FSSAI regulations.”
Not:
“Permitted in all foods.”
Rule 4: Evidence Must Be Separated from Regulation
Scientific evidence and regulatory status must never be mixed.
Example:
“Regulators require a warning label in the EU/UK.”
“Scientific evidence suggests a small effect on activity and attention in some children.”
These are separate statements.

4. Status Taxonomy
Use these exact status values.
Status
	
Meaning


Authorised
	
Permitted under specified conditions


Authorised with warning label
	
Permitted but warning label required


Restricted
	
Permitted only in certain categories or limits


Not authorised
	
Not permitted for food use in that jurisdiction


Not listed
	
Not found in official permitted list


Certification required
	
Colour must be certified before use


Under review
	
Regulatory review in progress


Unknown
	
Not yet verified

8. Product Flag Rules for Artificial Colours
When a scanned product contains an artificial colour, FoodLens AI must generate structured flags.
Flag 1: Artificial Colour Detected
Display:
“This product contains an artificial food colour.”
Flag 2: Southampton Six Colour Detected
Display:
“This product contains {colour name}, an artificial colour that requires a warning label in the EU/UK.”
Example:
“This product contains Sunset Yellow FCF, an artificial colour that requires a warning label in the EU/UK.”
Flag 3: Colour Not FDA-Certified in U.S.
Display only after verification:
“This colour is not listed as an FDA-certified food colour in the United States.”
Flag 4: Colour Permitted in India
Display only after FSSAI category verification:
“This colour is permitted in India under FSSAI regulations for specified food categories.”
Flag 5: Colour Restricted by Food Category
Display:
“This colour is permitted only in certain food categories and may be subject to maximum permitted levels.”
Flag 6: Evidence Uncertainty
Display:
“Scientific evidence for this colour is limited and should be interpreted with caution.”
9. Consumer-Facing Ingredient Card Template for Colours
Every artificial colour card must follow this structure.
Colour Card Example: Sunset Yellow FCF
Sunset Yellow FCF
Also known as: E110, INS 110, Yellow 6
What it is:
An artificial food colour used to give foods and drinks an orange-yellow colour.
Why manufacturers use it:
Improves product colour
Makes colour consistent across batches
Compensates for colour loss during storage
Regulatory status:
Region
	
Status


EU
	
Authorised, subject to conditions


UK
	
Authorised, subject to conditions


EU/UK warning label
	
Required


United States
	
FDA-certified colour additive; verify exact status


India
	
Verify FSSAI food category permission


Codex
	
Verify Codex GSFA permissions
Scientific evidence:
EFSA evaluated the Southampton study and found limited evidence of a small effect on activity and attention in some children .
Rare sensitivity reactions have been reported in susceptible individuals.
Evidence confidence:
Limited to moderate evidence.
Product flag:
“Contains Sunset Yellow FCF, an artificial colour that requires a warning label in the EU/UK.”

For each colour:

Record permitted food categories
Record maximum permitted levels
Record restrictions
Save source URL
Task 2: UK Database Extraction
For each colour:
Open UK approved additives list
Search E number
Record UK status
Record permitted categories
Record warning label requirement
Save source URL
Task 3: FDA Extraction
For each colour:
Open FDA Color Additive Status
Search colour name
Record whether FDA-certified for food
Record certification requirements
Record restrictions
Save source URL
Task 4: FSSAI Extraction
For each colour:
Open FSSAI compendium
Search INS number or colour name
Record permitted food categories
Record maximum permitted levels
Record labelling requirements
Save source URL
Task 5: Codex Extraction
For each colour:
Open Codex GSFA Online
Search INS number
Record Codex permissions
Record food categories
Record maximum use levels
Save source URL
Task 6: ADI Extraction
For each colour:
Open JECFA or EFSA evaluation
Record ADI value
Record year of evaluation
Record uncertainty factors
Save source URL
12. Batch 2 Acceptance Criteria
Batch 2 is complete only when all of the following are true.
Data Completeness
All 13 colours have registry entries
Every colour has E number and INS number
Every colour has EU status
Every colour has UK status
Every colour has U.S. FDA status
Every colour has India FSSAI status
Every colour has Codex status
Every colour has warning-label status
Every colour has at least one official source URL
Every colour has verification status
Regulatory Accuracy
Southampton Six warning label status is verified
No colour is called “banned” without official source
Every “not authorised” claim has official source
Every “warning label required” claim has official source
Every food-category restriction is recorded
Every maximum permitted level is recorded where available
Evidence Accuracy
Child behaviour evidence is labelled as limited where appropriate
Scientific evidence is separated from regulatory status
Evidence confidence is assigned
Conflicting evidence is clearly marked
No medical claims are made
Product Flag Accuracy
Product containing E102 triggers Southampton Six flag
Product containing E110 triggers Southampton Six flag
Product containing E129 triggers Southampton Six flag
Product containing E133 does not trigger Southampton Six flag
Product containing unknown colour triggers “unknown colour” flag
Product flags are neutral and non-emotional
13. Example Product Output Using Batch 2 Data
Product: Orange Soft Drink
Detected Colour
Sunset Yellow FCF, E110, INS 110
At-a-Glance Flag
“Contains Sunset Yellow FCF, an artificial colour that requires a warning label in the EU/UK.”
Ingredient Card Summary
Sunset Yellow FCF
An artificial food colour used to give drinks an orange-yellow colour.
Regulatory note:
This colour is authorised in the EU and UK, but foods containing it must carry a warning label in those jurisdictions.
Scientific note:
Studies evaluated by EFSA suggest a possible small effect on activity and attention in some children, but the evidence is limited.
Confidence:
Moderate regulatory confidence; limited scientific evidence.
14. Batch 2 Risk Controls
Risk 1: Incorrect “Banned” Claim
Prevention:
Use “not authorised” unless official source says banned
Require official source URL
Human review for all “banned” claims
Risk 2: Incorrect FDA Status
Prevention:
Extract directly from FDA Color Additive Status
Do not assume U.S. status from EU status
Mark uncertain entries as “verify”
Risk 3: Incorrect FSSAI Status
Prevention:
Extract from FSSAI compendium
Record food category
Record maximum permitted level
Do not say “permitted in India” without category check
Risk 4: Overstating Child Behaviour Evidence
Prevention:
Use “limited evidence” where appropriate
Do not say “causes hyperactivity”
Say “may affect activity and attention in some children”
Risk 5: Missing Warning Label Jurisdiction
Prevention:
Store warning label jurisdictions separately
Verify EU and UK rules
Add future jurisdictions only with official sources

Batch 2 is now structured as a build-ready Artificial Colour Registry.

EU Food Additives Database
UK Approved Additives list
